## 任务：Round 3 审稿修复 — Theoretical Analysis + Experiments

论文路径：`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_1/projects/cost-verify-agent-rl/docs/paper/`

先 Read `/home/ubuntu/agent-ml-research/skills/global/paper-writing-norms.skill.md` 了解写作规范。

### 1. Theoretical Analysis (`theoretical_analysis.tex`)

#### 1a. Proposition 2 改为 upper bound
当前 Proposition 2（约 line 14-18）写的是等式 `P(correct|update) = p/(1-(1-p)^K)`，并称 "only 19.1% of gradient updates have the correct sign"。

问题：Proposition 隐含假设 "episode 成功⟹所有窗口局部正确"，这只是上界（实际成功 episode 可能包含非最优窗口）。

修改：
- 等式改为 `≤`：`P(\text{correct} \mid \text{update}) \leq \frac{p}{1 - (1-p)^{K}}`
- 文字改为 "yielding an upper bound of 19.1% at p=12.5%, K=8"
- Proposition 文字也相应调整，加 "upper bound" 修饰
- Figure 2 caption 中对应的 "only 19.1%" 也改为 "at most 19.1%"
- "Severity scales with task difficulty" 段落中的数值也要加 "at most" 修饰

#### 1b. Eq.6 caveat（约 line 35-40 "Cumulative divergence" 段落）
当前提到 per-group advantage magnitude ratio ≈ 1.32。这是单点测量（单 seed、单训练步）。在提到 1.32 的句子后加：
"(measured from a single training snapshot; validation across seeds and training stages would strengthen this estimate)"

### 2. Experiments (`experiments.tex`)

#### 2a. Section 4.1 reward description fix（约 line 9）
当前写 "Reward is binary: R ∈ {0, r+}"，但实际实验使用 penalty reward（-0.1 per invalid action）。这与 "binary" 矛盾。

修改方案：在 Setup 段落中明确说明：
- 主实验使用 penalty reward structure: R ∈ {r_-, 0, r+}，其中 r_- = -0.1 per invalid action
- 理论分析（Section 3）假设纯 binary reward（R ∈ {0, r+}）作为分析上界
- Section 4.4 的 binary reward 验证实验（penalty=0）证实理论预测

具体措辞建议（你可以调整）：
"The environment provides ternary reward: $R \in \{r_-, 0, r_+\}$, where $r_- = -0.1$ per invalid action, $0$ for valid but unsuccessful actions, and $r_+$ for task success. Our theoretical analysis (Section~\ref{sec:theory}) assumes the simplified binary case ($R \in \{0, r_+\}$), which yields an upper bound on sign misattribution severity; Section~\ref{sec:sign_verification} validates this bound under both reward structures."

同时检查 method.tex line 7 "binary reward R ∈ {0, r+}" 是否也需要修改。如果 method.tex 是定义问题的 general formulation，可以保留 binary 但加一句说明实验中使用的是 penalty variant。

#### 2b. Section 4.4 三指标区分（约 line 59-64）
当前论文混淆了三个不同指标。修改 Section 4.4 使之清晰区分：

1. **Sign-episode correlation**：advantage sign 是否跟随 episode outcome（成功/失败）。Binary reward 下 100%（157/157），这是 by construction（因为所有成功 episode 窗口 return > 0）。这直接证明 M2 机制。
2. **Credit agreement rate**：advantage sign 是否匹配 local action quality（用 action validity 作为 proxy）。Penalty reward 下测得 52.07%（n=3000），接近随机 50%。这说明 advantage sign 几乎不携带 local action quality 信息。
3. **P(correct|update)**：Proposition 2 的理论上界 ≤19.1%，是不同指标，不能与 52% 直接比较。

修改要点：
- 重写 Section 4.4 开头，先定义 "credit agreement rate" 的 operationalization（当前已有但需要更清晰）
- 52% credit agreement 说明 advantage sign 几乎不携带 local action quality 信息（接近随机 50%）
- Binary reward 100% sign-episode correlation 证明 M2（advantage sign 完全由 episode outcome 决定），而不是验证 Proposition 2 的 19.1%
- 19.1% 是 P(correct|update) 的理论上界，与 52% credit agreement 是不同指标

当前文本中 "52.07%...marginally above random chance (p=0.025 vs. 50%; +2.07pp effect size)" — 这个统计检验是正确的，保留。但要明确这测的是 credit agreement（action quality proxy），不是 P(correct|update)。

#### 2c. Table 1 标注（约 line 33-52）
在 Table 1 caption 中添加：
- Cell D 的 11-seed study 交叉引用："Cell~D results are consistent with the 11-seed evaluation (Table~\ref{tab:seed_variability}), where mean OOD$=$17.2\% with 36\% generalization rate."
- 明确标注每个 cell 的 seed 数量：在 caption 或 table 中说明 "Cell A/B: 2 seeds each; Cell D: 2 seeds (mean reported); Dense reward/REINFORCE++: 1 seed each"

#### 2d. Cell C 重框架化
当前 Cell C 只说 "encountered persistent training instability (Appendix)"。添加一句分析：

在 Section 4.2 讨论 Cell C 的地方（约 line 14），或在 Section 4.3 Results 末尾，添加：
"Cell~C's NaN instability---qualitatively different from Cell~A's gradual collapse---suggests that information loss may have an unintended stabilizing effect on gradient variance, and that the M1--M2 interaction may be non-additive (Section~\ref{sec:discussion})."

#### 2e. 匿名性修复
检查 experiments.tex 中的 footnote（约 line 9 附近），如果有 GitHub URL（如 `https://github.com/RAGEN-AI/RAGEN`），替换为：
"Code available at \url{https://anonymous.4open.science/r/cost-verify-agent-rl}" 或 "Code available upon acceptance."

### 验证
修改完成后检查：
1. reward description 在 setup 中准确描述了 ternary reward
2. Section 4.4 清晰区分了三个指标
3. Table 1 有 seed count 和 Table 2 交叉引用
4. Cell C 有分析性评论
5. 无非匿名 URL

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01XgH2mskA62iz2yLDCyArgx`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\subsection{Problem Formulation and GRPO}
5	\label{sec:problem_formulation}
6	
7	We model the multi-turn agent as a partially observable Markov decision process (POMDP) where the policy $\pi_\phi(a_t \mid h_t)$ generates actions conditioned on interaction history $h_t = (o_1, a_1, \ldots, o_t)$. Episodes of $T$ steps terminate with binary reward $R \in \{0, r_+\}$. GRPO~\cite{shao2024deepseekmath} samples $K$ rollouts per task and computes group-relative advantages:
8	\begin{align}
9	\hat{A}_k &= \frac{R_k - \mu}{\sigma + \epsilon}, \notag \\
10	\mu &= \frac{1}{K}\sum_{j=1}^K R_j, \quad \sigma = \sqrt{\frac{1}{K}\sum_{j=1}^K (R_j - \mu)^2}
11	\label{eq:grpo_advantage}
12	\end{align}
13	GRPO is episode-level by design: each rollout receives one return and one advantage, with no inherent temporal discounting.
14	
15	\subsection{Step-Independent Windowing}
16	\label{sec:step_independent}
17	
18	Agent RL frameworks must reconcile GRPO's episode-level design with the memory constraints of long trajectories. RAGEN~\cite{ragen2025} introduces \textbf{step-independent training}: the trajectory is partitioned into non-overlapping $h$-step windows, each receiving a discounted return:
19	\begin{equation}
20	R_k = \gamma^{(N - k)} \cdot R_{\text{episode}}
21	\label{eq:step_return}
22	\end{equation}
23	where $N = \lceil T/h \rceil$ is the number of windows. Each window is optimized as an independent GRPO sample. This reduces sequence length from $O(T)$ to $O(h)$ and multiplies the effective batch size, but structurally alters credit assignment.
24	
25	\begin{figure*}[t]
26	\centering
27	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{fig_overview.pdf}
28	\caption{Step-independent windowing (left) partitions the trajectory into fixed windows, each optimized independently. This introduces two failure mechanisms: M1 (information loss) truncates context at window boundaries, and M2 (sign misattribution) assigns all windows the same advantage sign based on episode outcome. Episode-level training (right) treats the full trajectory as a single sample, eliminating both mechanisms.}
29	\label{fig:overview}
30	\end{figure*}
31	
32	\subsection{Dual Mechanism Analysis}
33	\label{sec:dual_mechanism}
34	
35	Two mechanisms jointly cause training failure under step-independent optimization.
36	
37	\subsubsection{Mechanism 1: Policy Information Loss}
38	\label{sec:m1_information_loss}
39	
40	Step-independent training truncates context at both inference and training time. The policy $\pi_\phi(a_t \mid h_{\text{window}_k})$ conditions on only the local $h$-step history, losing access to earlier observations. With $h{=}2$, a window at step~8 has no access to the outcome of an exploration action at step~4, so the policy cannot learn strategies spanning more than $h$ steps. This imposes a \textit{constant} performance ceiling: no amount of optimization can recover absent information.
41	
42	\subsubsection{Mechanism 2: Advantage Sign Misattribution}
43	\label{sec:m2_sign_misattribution}
44	
45	Group normalization under step-independent training produces advantages whose sign reflects the \textit{episode} outcome, not local action quality:
46	\begin{equation}
47	\text{sign}(\hat{A}_k) \approx \text{sign}(R_{\text{episode}} - \mu)
48	\label{eq:sign_correlation}
49	\end{equation}
50	In failed episodes ($R_{\text{episode}} = 0$), all windows receive negative advantages when $\mu > 0$, including windows with locally correct actions. Conversely, successful episodes reinforce all windows, including those with suboptimal actions. Unlike M1's constant ceiling, sign misattribution is \textit{cumulative}: each wrong-sign gradient step further erodes policy competence. The discount factor $\gamma^{(N-k)}$ introduces magnitude decay across positions, but sign errors are strictly more harmful: magnitude decay slows convergence, while sign errors cause active divergence.
51	
52	\subsection{Episode-Level Training}
53	\label{sec:episode_level}
54	
55	Episode-level training addresses both mechanisms by treating the complete trajectory as a single GRPO sample. The full history $h_t$ is available at every step (eliminating M1), and a single advantage $\hat{A}$ applies uniformly to all tokens (eliminating M2). Uniform reinforcement of successful trajectories preserves good sub-strategies; window-level sign errors actively destroy them. This is GRPO's original design; step-independent windowing is the deviation.
56	
57	\subsection{Factorial Experiment Design}
58	\label{sec:factorial_design}
59	
60	To separate the two mechanisms, we implement a $2 \times 2$ factorial varying observation scope and advantage granularity: \textbf{Cell~A} (truncated $h{=}2$ + step-level advantage; both mechanisms active), \textbf{Cell~B} (truncated $h{=}2$ + episode-level advantage; only M1 active), and \textbf{Cell~D} (full trajectory + episode-level advantage; neither active). The fourth cell, \textbf{Cell~C} (full trajectory + step-level advantage; only M2 active), would isolate sign misattribution without information loss. We attempted this configuration but encountered persistent training instability (Section~\ref{sec:factorial_experiments}; Appendix~\ref{app:cell_c}). Comparing A vs.\ B isolates sign misattribution; comparing B vs.\ D isolates information loss.
61	
```

````text
1	---
2	name: paper-writing-norms
3	description: 顶会级别论文写作风格规范——章节写法（Abstract/Intro/Related Work）、去 AI 风格、视觉密度、叙事哲学。Paper Agent 和 Worker 写论文章节时必须加载。
4	allowed-tools: [Read, Write, Edit, Glob, Grep]
5	scope: global
6	version: 2
7	priority: 9
8	tags:
9	  - writing
10	  - paper
11	  - style
12	  - norms
13	roles:
14	  - agent
15	  - worker
16	  - paper_agent
17	---
18	
19	# 论文写作风格规范
20	
21	> 核心哲学：**论文是一个有说服力的故事，不是实验报告。** 审稿人在几十秒内判断论文价值——如果 Abstract 和 Introduction 读起来像实验日志，论文就已经失败了。
22	
23	---
24	
25	## 一、总体写作哲学
26	
27	### 1.1 故事驱动，不是数据堆砌
28	
29	- 论文必须以"故事线"为核心展开：问题 → 洞察 → 方法 → 验证
30	- 禁止大量实验数据堆砌——每个数字出现必须服务于叙事推进
31	- 附录内容必须在正文中有明确引用且融入叙事，禁止简单堆砌
32	- 如果附录数据对核心叙事有重大影响，根据附录数据调整论文 framing
33	
34	### 1.2 主线统一性
35	
36	- 当 idea 在研究过程中发生变化后，**必须重审全文逻辑**
37	- 常见问题：Introduction 讲的故事 A，Method 做的是故事 B，Results 又在讲故事 C
38	- 修复方式：从最终实验结论反推，重写 Introduction 的问题定义和动机
39	- 每次 idea 调整后检查：Abstract → Introduction → Method → Results → Discussion → Conclusion 是否形成一条连贯主线
40	
41	### 1.3 专有名词必须有定义
42	
43	- 首次出现的专有名词/缩写必须给出完整定义和解释
44	- Abstract 中使用的缩写也必须在 Abstract 内定义（如 LLM = Large Language Model）
45	- 不能假设读者知道你自己定义的术语——论文从来没解释过的概念不能直接使用
46	- 通用规则：如果一个非标准术语第一次出现时没有括号定义，就是写作 bug
47	
48	### 1.4 写作前必读同类优秀论文
49	
50	- 每个章节动笔前，找 **2-3 篇目标会议近 1-2 年的高分论文**作为风格参考——同类问题或同类方法皆可
51	- 重点学：
52	  - Abstract 怎么用最少篇幅讲完故事
53	  - Introduction 怎么从大问题切入到具体贡献
54	  - Related Work 怎么 positioning 自己（不是 survey）
55	  - Table/Figure 怎么压缩信息密度
56	- **不是抄袭**，是学习同行的故事节奏和审美——不同子领域叙事节奏差异很大
57	- 实操：Paper Agent 可以从 `literature_map.yaml` 中挑 `role=foundation` 或 `role=direct-competitor` 的论文，把 `{title, venue, year, abstract}` 附在 Worker 的 task_prompt 中作为概念参考；如需学习写作风格本身，让 Worker 通过 WebSearch 取得论文链接并 fetch 全文
58	
59	---
60	
61	## 二、Abstract 写作规范
62	
63	### 2.1 四个必答问题（150-250 words）
64	
65	Abstract 必须在很短的篇幅内清楚回答：
66	1. **研究什么问题** — 1-2 句话交代背景和痛点
67	2. **为什么重要** — 现有方法的不足（不用写太多）
68	3. **本文提出什么** — 核心贡献的关键机制（简要，不堆砌细节）
69	4. **结果说明什么** — 用具体实验结论支撑贡献
70	
71	### 2.2 禁忌
72	
73	- **禁止堆砌数据**：Abstract 不是 Results 的缩写。最多引用 2-3 个关键数字，要服务于 claim
74	- **禁止过度宣传**：不说 "revolutionary"、"groundbreaking"
75	- **禁止写成 Introduction 的缩写**：Abstract 是独立阅读单元
76	- **禁止术语堆积**：如果审稿人几十秒内判断不出价值，就失败了
77	- **缩写必须定义**：Abstract 中首次出现的缩写必须展开
78	
79	### 2.3 审稿人视角检验
80	
81	写完后用这个标准自检：审稿人能否在 30 秒内判断——
82	- 问题是否重要？
83	- 方法是否清楚？
84	- 贡献是否可信？
85	- 结果是否有说服力？
86	
87	---
88	
89	## 三、Introduction 写作规范
90	
91	### 3.1 完整叙事线（5 段结构）
92	
93	Introduction 需要从一个清晰、重要的问题切入，逐步把读者带到核心贡献：
94	
95	1. **研究背景**（1-2 段）：大模型在某类能力/应用中已取得进展，但仍存在一个关键瓶颈
96	2. **问题重要性**（1 段）：结合真实应用、已有方法局限或当前趋势，说明这不是小问题
97	3. **现有方法不足**（1 段）：系统梳理为什么不够（依赖更大模型/更多数据/更复杂推理/更高成本，或只在特定 benchmark 有效）
98	4. **本文核心想法**（1 段）：我们观察到什么、提出了什么方法、为什么能解决上述问题
99	5. **贡献总结**（列表）：清楚列出方法贡献、实验贡献和分析贡献
100	
101	### 3.2 整体叙事结构
102	
103	```
104	现有进展 → 关键缺口 → 本文洞察 → 方法概述 → 实验证据 → 贡献总结
105	```
106	
107	### 3.3 禁忌
108	
109	- **禁止泛泛介绍**：不要开头就写 "LLMs have shown remarkable capabilities..."，要从具体问题切入
110	- **禁止缺少过渡**：每段之间必须有逻辑连接
111	- **禁止实验报告风格**：Introduction 不是 "我们做了 X 实验得到了 Y 结果" 的罗列
112	- 每条 Contribution 必须有对应的 section/table 支撑
113	- Contribution 列表中不能出现未在前文铺垫过的概念
114	
115	---
116	
117	## 四、Related Work 写作规范
118	
119	### 4.1 目标
120	
121	让审稿人相信：作者理解这个领域，知道自己的方法站在什么位置，清楚相对已有工作的真实增量在哪里。
122	
123	### 4.2 结构要求
124	
125	- 把已有工作分成几个清晰的主题（如模型训练、推理增强、工具调用、数据构建、评测等）
126	- 每类简要说明：已有方法做了什么、解决了哪些问题、和本文的关系
127	- 帮助读者理解：哪些是本文直接继承的，哪些方法不同但解决相似问题，哪些留下了本文要解决的缺口
128	
129	### 4.3 禁忌
130	
131	- **禁止论文堆砌**：不要写成 "A proposed X. B proposed Y. C proposed Z." 的列表
132	- **目标不是展示读了多少论文**，而是做归纳和比较
133	- Contribution claim（"To our knowledge, no prior work..."）应放在 Introduction，不在 Related Work
134	
135	### 4.4 引用充足度
136	
137	- 引用必须覆盖：**直接相关方法、baseline 论文、评测 benchmark 论文、理论基础**
138	- 任何一类缺失，审稿人会立刻看出来调研不充分
139	- 引用密度参照目标会议近年录用论文的量级——明显偏少意味着 Related Work 调研不足
140	
141	---
142	
143	## 五、去 AI 写作风格（硬规则）
144	
145	以下模式是 AI 生成文本的典型标志，审稿人一眼能看出。**必须全部消除。**
146	
147	### 5.1 严禁破折号（最显著的 AI 痕迹）
148	
149	**这是 AI 写作最容易被识别的标志，审稿人最敏感。单列为最高优先级硬规则。**
150	
151	- 解释性 em-dash（—，U+2014）和 en-dash（–，U+2013）必须**全部删除**
152	- 替代方式：用逗号、括号或拆分为两个句子
153	- 强制检查命令：
154	  ```bash
155	  grep -n "—" paper/*.tex     # em-dash，每处人工判断是否必要
156	  grep -n " – " paper/*.tex   # 解释性 en-dash，几乎都应删除
157	  ```
158	- 例外（允许保留）：表格分隔符、数字范围（`2020–2024`、`pp. 12–18`）、专有名词（`Newton–Raphson`）
159	
160	### 5.2 段落与句式
161	
162	- **禁止过度分段**：不需要每 2-3 句就换段，自然段 5-8 句是正常的
163	- **减少关系从句**（which, that, who）：能用简洁直接的句子就不用从句
164	- **禁止 -ing 短语结尾**：如 "..., highlighting the applicability of..." / "..., demonstrating the effectiveness of..."。这是 AI 写作最典型的标志之一
165	- **禁止连续使用 "Furthermore" / "Moreover" / "Additionally"**：一段内最多用一个过渡词
166	- **避免 "It is worth noting that..." / "Notably, ..."**：直接写要说的内容
167	
168	### 5.3 用词黑名单
169	
170	- 避免 "delve into" / "leverage" / "utilize" / "facilitate" — 用 "explore" / "use" / "enable"
171	- 避免 "a myriad of" / "a plethora of" — 用 "many" / "various"
172	- 避免 "in the realm of" / "in the context of" — 直接说
173	- 避免 "paradigm shift" / "novel" 过度使用
174	
175	### 5.4 校对清单（4 步）
176	
177	1. 全文 proofread 语法和拼写
178	2. 检查所有代词/指示词（this, that, it）是否指代明确
179	3. **`grep "—"` + `grep " – "` 全文清零**（删除所有解释性 dash，§5.1）
180	4. `grep "ing,"` 模式——大部分 -ing 分词短语需要改写
181	
182	---
183	
184	## 六、图表与视觉规范
185	
186	### 6.1 知识密度原则
187	
188	- **图表的信息必须紧凑**——不能为了美观而留大量白空间
189	- 一张图如果只传达一个简单信息，要么缩小要么合并到其他图中
190	- 能用 inline text 说清的不要占一整个 Figure
191	
192	### 6.2 表格规范
193	
194	- 表格和正文文字不能视觉重叠（检查 column 宽度和 alignment）
195	- 小数精度全表统一
196	- 最优加粗、次优下划线
197	- Caption 必须自包含
198	
199	### 6.3 图片规范
200	
201	- 数值标签不能被线、bar、点遮住——用白色背景或偏移标签
202	- 图中字体 >= 7pt（打印后仍可读）
203	- 栏宽约束需匹配目标 venue 模板要求
204	- 概念图/流程图要有信息量，不要画一个 trivial 的 pipeline 然后占半页
205	
206	### 6.4 空间效率
207	
208	- 顶会正文页数严格受限（具体页数查目标会议官网），每 cm² 都是宝贵资源
209	- 优先使用 `\subfigure` 合并相关图
210	- Table 能 inline 就不要单独浮动
211	- 图表占比与其对叙事的贡献成正比
212	
213	### 6.5 页面节奏
214	
215	好论文的页面有节奏感——text、equation、figure 交替出现，读者视觉不疲劳：
216	
217	- **text-equation-figure 三重奏**：重要概念用"文字解释 → 公式定义 → 图表直觉"三步展开
218	- 连续 2 页纯文本（无 figure/equation/table）→ 穿插一个 display item 打破节奏
219	- 连续 2 个 figure 页（整页被 float 占满）→ 至少隔 1 段文字再放下一个 float
220	- Section heading 不孤悬页底（heading 后至少跟 2 行正文，否则强制分页）
221	- 最后一页内容 ≥ 2/3 页面高度（太空显得凑数，太满放不下 references）
222	
223	### 6.6 表格 LaTeX 规范
224	
225	**Booktabs 标准（硬规则）**：
226	- 用 `\toprule` / `\midrule` / `\bottomrule`，禁止 `\hline`，禁止竖线 `|`
227	- 用 `\centering` 不用 `\begin{center}`（后者多加垂直空白）
228	- 最优结果加粗（`\textbf{}`），次优下划线（`\underline{}`）+ 方向箭头 `↑`/`↓` 标明指标方向
229	- 小数精度全表统一（如全部保留 1 位或 2 位）
230	- Caption 在表格上方（`\caption` 在 `\begin{tabular}` 之前）
231	
232	**宽度决策树**：
233	- ≤5 列 → 单栏宽度（`\columnwidth`）
234	- 6–8 列 → 全文宽度（`\textwidth`，双栏模板用 `table*`）
235	- >8 列 → 拆成两张表或移入 appendix
236	
237	---
238	
239	## 七、投稿前通用准备
240	
241	### 7.1 模板必须官方（硬规则）
242	
243	- LaTeX 模板**必须从目标会议官网下载**（CallForPapers / Author Instructions 页面）
244	- **不要用**：
245	  - 网上他人转载的复制版本（可能被改过 margin / 行号设置）
246	  - 上一年的模板（参数可能变：字体、margin、行号、anonymous 选项等）
247	  - 组里学长留的旧版本（来源不明、无法追溯）
248	- 风险：
249	  - 样式不符合 → desk reject
250	  - margin 不对 → 被审稿人质疑诚信
251	  - reference 格式错 → 被认为不规范
252	- 流程：先查会议官网 templates → 找不到再 `WebSearch "{venue} {year} latex template official"` → 仍找不到汇报 Director（不要用就近年版本顶替）
253	- `\documentclass[]{}` 选项必须与目标 venue + 阶段（review / camera-ready）一致
254	
255	### 7.2 投稿要求逐项核对
256	
257	- 目标会议如要求 Checklist / Ethical Statement / Broader Impact Statement / Reproducibility Statement，必须**认真填每一项**，不要全写 N/A
258	- 页数限制：查目标会议官网
259	- 匿名化要求：review 版 vs camera-ready 版本设置不同
260	- 行号开启（review 版常需）
261	- 字体大小、margin 不得修改 sty 文件
262	
263	### 7.3 最终修改 10 项检查流程
264	
265	提交前必须逐一检查：
266	1. 所有最新实验结果已全部录入
267	2. 根据实验结果将叙事调至最优状态
268	3. 附录所有内容在正文中被引用且融入叙事
269	4. 无数据堆砌（每个数字服务于叙事推进）
270	5. Abstract 和 Introduction 具有吸引力（不是实验报告风格）
271	6. 全文主线统一（idea 变化后各章节逻辑一致）
272	7. 所有专有名词有定义
273	8. 无 AI 风格标志（dash、-ing phrases、过度分段）
274	9. 图表知识密度高、无视觉遮挡
275	10. Reference 覆盖直接相关方法、baseline、benchmark、理论基础
276	
````

</details>

      "content": "---\nname: paper-writing-norms\ndescription: 顶会级别论文写作风格规范——章节写法（Abstract/Intro/Related Work）、去 AI 风格、视觉密度、叙事哲学。Paper Agent 和 Worker 写论文章节时必须加载。\nallowed-tools: [Read, Write, Edit, Glob, Grep]\nscope: global\nversion: 2\npriority: 9\ntags:\n  - writing\n  - paper\n  - style\n  - norms\nroles:\n  - agent\n  - worker\n  - paper_agent\n---\n\n# 论文写作风格规范\n\n> 核心哲学：**论文是一个有说服力的故事，不是实验报告。** 审稿人在几十秒内判断论文价值——如果 Abstract 和 Introduction 读起来像实验日志，论文就已经失败了。\n\n---\n\n## 一、总体写作哲学\n\n### 1.1 故事驱动，不是数据堆砌\n\n- 论文必须以\"故事线\"为核心展开：问题 → 洞察 → 方法 → 验证\n- 禁止大量实验数据堆砌——每个数字出现必须服务于叙事推进\n- 附录内容必须在正文中有明确引用且融入叙事，禁止简单堆砌\n- 如果附录数据对核心叙事有重大影响，根据附录数据调整论文 framing\n\n### 1.2 主线统一性\n\n- 当 idea 在研究过程中发生变化后，**必须重审全文逻辑**\n- 常见问题：Introduction 讲的故事 A，Method 做的是故事 B，Results 又在讲故事 C\n- 修复方式：从最终实验结论反推，重写 Introduction 的问题定义和动机\n- 每次 idea 调整后检查：Abstract → Introduction → Method → Results → Discussion → Conclusion 是否形成一条连贯主线\n\n### 1.3 专有名词必须有定义\n\n- 首次出现的专有名词/缩写必须给出完整定义和解释\n- Abstract 中使用的缩写也必须在 Abstract 内定义（如 LLM = Large Language Model）\n- 不能假设读者知道你自己定义的术语——论文从来没解释过的概念不能直接使用\n- 通用规则：如果一个非标准术语第一次出现时没有括号定义，就是写作 bug\n\n### 1.4 写作前必读同类优秀论文\n\n- 每个章节动笔前，找 **2-3 篇目标会议近 1-2 年的高分论文**作为风格参考——同类问题或同类方法皆可\n- 重点学：\n  - Abstract 怎么用最少篇幅讲完故事\n  - Introduction 怎么从大问题切入到具体贡献\n  - Related Work 怎么 positioning 自己（不是 survey）\n  - Table/Figure 怎么压缩信息密度\n- **不是抄袭**，是学习同行的故事节奏和审美——不同子领域叙事节奏差异很大\n- 实操：Paper Agent 可以从 `literature_map.yaml` 中挑 `role=foundation` 或 `role=direct-competitor` 的论文，把 `{title, venue, year, abstract}` 附在 Worker 的 task_prompt 中作为概念参考；如需学习写作风格本身，让 Worker 通过 WebSearch 取得论文链接并 fetch 全文\n\n---\n\n## 二、Abstract 写作规范\n\n### 2.1 四个必答问题（150-250 words）\n\nAbstract 必须在很短的篇幅内清楚回答：\n1. **研究什么问题** — 1-2 句话交代背景和痛点\n2. **为什么重要** — 现有方法的不足（不用写太多）\n3. **本文提出什么** — 核心贡献的关键机制（简要，不堆砌细节）\n4. **结果说明什么** — 用具体实验结论支撑贡献\n\n### 2.2 禁忌\n\n- **禁止堆砌数据**：Abstract 不是 Results 的缩写。最多引用 2-3 个关键数字，要服务于 claim\n- **禁止过度宣传**：不说 \"revolutionary\"、\"groundbreaking\"\n- **禁止写成 Introduction 的缩写**：Abstract 是独立阅读单元\n- **禁止术语堆积**：如果审稿人几十秒内判断不出价值，就失败了\n- **缩写必须定义**：Abstract 中首次出现的缩写必须展开\n\n### 2.3 审稿人视角检验\n\n写完后用这个标准自检：审稿人能否在 30 秒内判断——\n- 问题是否重要？\n- 方法是否清楚？\n- 贡献是否可信？\n- 结果是否有说服力？\n\n---\n\n## 三、Introduction 写作规范\n\n### 3.1 完整叙事线（5 段结构）\n\nIntroduction 需要从一个清晰、重要的问题切入，逐步把读者带到核心贡献：\n\n1. **研究背景**（1-2 段）：大模型在某类能力/应用中已取得进展，但仍存在一个关键瓶颈\n2. **问题重要性**（1 段）：结合真实应用、已有方法局限或当前趋势，说明这不是小问题\n3. **现有方法不足**（1 段）：系统梳理为什么不够（依赖更大模型/更多数据/更复杂推理/更高成本，或只在特定 benchmark 有效）\n4. **本文核心想法**（1 段）：我们观察到什么、提出了什么方法、为什么能解决上述问题\n5. **贡献总结**（列表）：清楚列出方法贡献、实验贡献和分析贡献\n\n### 3.2 整体叙事结构\n\n```\n现有进展 → 关键缺口 → 本文洞察 → 方法概述 → 实验证据 → 贡献总结\n```\n\n### 3.3 禁忌\n\n- **禁止泛泛介绍**：不要开头就写 \"LLMs have shown remarkable capabilities...\"，要从具体问题切入\n- **禁止缺少过渡**：每段之间必须有逻辑连接\n- **禁止实验报告风格**：Introduction 不是 \"我们做了 X 实验得到了 Y 结果\" 的罗列\n- 每条 Contribution 必须有对应的 section/table 支撑\n- Contribution 列表中不能出现未在前文铺垫过的概念\n\n---\n\n## 四、Related Work 写作规范\n\n### 4.1 目标\n\n让审稿人相信：作者理解这个领域，知道自己的方法站在什么位置，清楚相对已有工作的真实增量在哪里。\n\n### 4.2 结构要求\n\n- 把已有工作分成几个清晰的主题（如模型训练、推理增强、工具调用、数据构建、评测等）\n- 每类简要说明：已有方法做了什么、解决了哪些问题、和本文的关系\n- 帮助读者理解：哪些是本文直接继承的，哪些方法不同但解决相似问题，哪些留下了本文要解决的缺口\n\n### 4.3 禁忌\n\n- **禁止论文堆砌**：不要写成 \"A proposed X. B proposed Y. C proposed Z.\" 的列表\n- **目标不是展示读了多少论文**，而是做归纳和比较\n- Contribution claim（\"To our knowledge, no prior work...\"）应放在 Introduction，不在 Related Work\n\n### 4.4 引用充足度\n\n- 引用必须覆盖：**直接相关方法、baseline 论文、评测 benchmark 论文、理论基础**\n- 任何一类缺失，审稿人会立刻看出来调研不充分\n- 引用密度参照目标会议近年录用论文的量级——明显偏少意味着 Related Work 调研不足\n\n---\n\n## 五、去 AI 写作风格（硬规则）\n\n以下模式是 AI 生成文本的典型标志，审稿人一眼能看出。**必须全部消除。**\n\n### 5.1 严禁破折号（最显著的 AI 痕迹）\n\n**这是 AI 写作最容易被识别的标志，审稿人最敏感。单列为最高优先级硬规则。**\n\n- 解释性 em-dash（—，U+2014）和 en-dash（–，U+2013）必须**全部删除**\n- 替代方式：用逗号、括号或拆分为两个句子\n- 强制检查命令：\n  ```bash\n  grep -n \"—\" paper/*.tex     # em-dash，每处人工判断是否必要\n  grep -n \" – \" paper/*.tex   # 解释性 en-dash，几乎都应删除\n  ```\n- 例外（允许保留）：表格分隔符、数字范围（`2020–2024`、`pp. 12–18`）、专有名词（`Newton–Raphson`）\n\n### 5.2 段落与句式\n\n- **禁止过度分段**：不需要每 2-3 句就换段，自然段 5-8 句是正常的\n- **减少关系从句**（which, that, who）：能用简洁直接的句子就不用从句\n- **禁止 -ing 短语结尾**：如 \"..., highlighting the applicability of...\" / \"..., demonstrating the effectiveness of...\"。这是 AI 写作最典型的标志之一\n- **禁止连续使用 \"Furthermore\" / \"Moreover\" / \"Additionally\"**：一段内最多用一个过渡词\n- **避免 \"It is worth noting that...\" / \"Notably, ...\"**：直接写要说的内容\n\n### 5.3 用词黑名单\n\n- 避免 \"delve into\" / \"leverage\" / \"utilize\" / \"facilitate\" — 用 \"explore\" / \"use\" / \"enable\"\n- 避免 \"a myriad of\" / \"a plethora of\" — 用 \"many\" / \"various\"\n- 避免 \"in the realm of\" / \"in the context of\" — 直接说\n- 避免 \"paradigm shift\" / \"novel\" 过度使用\n\n### 5.4 校对清单（4 步）\n\n1. 全文 proofread 语法和拼写\n2. 检查所有代词/指示词（this, that, it）是否指代明确\n3. **`grep \"—\"` + `grep \" – \"` 全文清零**（删除所有解释性 dash，§5.1）\n4. `grep \"ing,\"` 模式——大部分 -ing 分词短语需要改写\n\n---\n\n## 六、图表与视觉规范\n\n### 6.1 知识密度原则\n\n- **图表的信息必须紧凑**——不能为了美观而留大量白空间\n- 一张图如果只传达一个简单信息，要么缩小要么合并到其他图中\n- 能用 inline text 说清的不要占一整个 Figure\n\n### 6.2 表格规范\n\n- 表格和正文文字不能视觉重叠（检查 column 宽度和 alignment）\n- 小数精度全表统一\n- 最优加粗、次优下划线\n- Caption 必须自包含\n\n### 6.3 图片规范\n\n- 数值标签不能被线、bar、点遮住——用白色背景或偏移标签\n- 图中字体 >= 7pt（打印后仍可读）\n- 栏宽约束需匹配目标 venue 模板要求\n- 概念图/流程图要有信息量，不要画一个 trivial 的 pipeline 然后占半页\n\n### 6.4 空间效率\n\n- 顶会正文页数严格受限（具体页数查目标会议官网），每 cm² 都是宝贵资源\n- 优先使用 `\\subfigure` 合并相关图\n- Table 能 inline 就不要单独浮动\n- 图表占比与其对叙事的贡献成正比\n\n### 6.5 页面节奏\n\n好论文的页面有节奏感——text、equation、figure 交替出现，读者视觉不疲劳：\n\n- **text-equation-figure 三重奏**：重要概念用\"文字解释 → 公式定义 → 图表直觉\"三步展开\n- 连续 2 页纯文本（无 figure/equation/table）→ 穿插一个 display item 打破节奏\n- 连续 2 个 figure 页（整页被 float 占满）→ 至少隔 1 段文字再放下一个 float\n- Section heading 不孤悬页底（heading 后至少跟 2 行正文，否则强制分页）\n- 最后一页内容 ≥ 2/3 页面高度（太空显得凑数，太满放不下 references）\n\n### 6.6 表格 LaTeX 规范\n\n**Booktabs 标准（硬规则）**：\n- 用 `\\toprule` / `\\midrule` / `\\bottomrule`，禁止 `\\hline`，禁止竖线 `|`\n- 用 `\\centering` 不用 `\\begin{center}`（后者多加垂直空白）\n- 最优结果加粗（`\\textbf{}`），次优下划线（`\\underline{}`）+ 方向箭头 `↑`/`↓` 标明指标方向\n- 小数精度全表统一（如全部保留 1 位或 2 位）\n- Caption 在表格上方（`\\caption` 在 `\\begin{tabular}` 之前）\n\n**宽度决策树**：\n- ≤5 列 → 单栏宽度（`\\columnwidth`）\n- 6–8 列 → 全文宽度（`\\textwidth`，双栏模板用 `table*`）\n- >8 列 → 拆成两张表或移入 appendix\n\n---\n\n## 七、投稿前通用准备\n\n### 7.1 模板必须官方（硬规则）\n\n- LaTeX 模板**必须从目标会议官网下载**（CallForPapers / Author Instructions 页面）\n- **不要用**：\n  - 网上他人转载的复制版本（可能被改过 margin / 行号设置）\n  - 上一年的模板（参数可能变：字体、margin、行号、anonymous 选项等）\n  - 组里学长留的旧版本（来源不明、无法追溯）\n- 风险：\n  - 样式不符合 → desk reject\n  - margin 不对 → 被审稿人质疑诚信\n  - reference 格式错 → 被认为不规范\n- 流程：先查会议官网 templates → 找不到再 `WebSearch \"{venue} {year} latex template official\"` → 仍找不到汇报 Director（不要用就近年版本顶替）\n- `\\documentclass[]{}` 选项必须与目标 venue + 阶段（review / camera-ready）一致\n\n### 7.2 投稿要求逐项核对\n\n- 目标会议如要求 Checklist / Ethical Statement / Broader Impact Statement / Reproducibility Statement，必须**认真填每一项**，不要全写 N/A\n- 页数限制：查目标会议官网\n- 匿名化要求：review 版 vs camera-ready 版本设置不同\n- 行号开启（review 版常需）\n- 字体大小、margin 不得修改 sty 文件\n\n### 7.3 最终修改 10 项检查流程\n\n提交前必须逐一检查：\n1. 所有最新实验结果已全部录入\n2. 根据实验结果将叙事调至最优状态\n3. 附录所有内容在正文中被引用且融入叙事\n4. 无数据堆砌（每个数字服务于叙事推进）\n5. Abstract 和 Introduction 具有吸引力（不是实验报告风格）\n6. 全文主线统一（idea 变化后各章节逻辑一致）\n7. 所有专有名词有定义\n8. 无 AI 风格标志（dash、-ing phrases、过度分段）\n9. 图表知识密度高、无视觉遮挡\n10. Reference 覆盖直接相关方法、baseline、benchmark、理论基础\n",

```text
1	\section{Theoretical Analysis}
2	\label{sec:theory}
3	
4	Sign misattribution is the more fundamental failure mechanism because it causes active divergence, not merely slow convergence. We derive the probability of incorrect gradient direction below; full proofs appear in Appendix~\ref{app:theory_full}.
5	
6	\textbf{Definition 1} (Locally correct action). \textit{An action $a_t$ is \textbf{locally correct} if it would receive positive advantage under an oracle that observes the full episode history $h_t$ and has access to the true action-value function. Equivalently, window $k$ contains locally correct actions if replacing them (holding all other windows fixed) would decrease the probability of episode success.}
7	
8	\textbf{Proposition 1.} \textit{Under step-independent training with binary episode reward $R_{\text{episode}} \in \{0, r_+\}$, the probability that window $k$'s advantage $\hat{A}_k$ has the correct sign (aligned with local action quality) approaches the base success rate $p$ as the number of rollouts $K$ per group increases.}
9	
10	\textit{Proof sketch.} In a failed episode ($R_{\text{episode}} = 0$), all windows receive $R_k = 0$. If any rollout in the group succeeded, $\mu > 0$, so every window in the failed episode receives $\hat{A}_k < 0$---including windows with locally correct actions. The probability of receiving the wrong sign in a failed episode is $1 - (1-p)^{K-1}$ (at least one group member succeeded). As $K$ grows, this approaches $1-p$, and the overall probability of correct sign approaches $p$. \hfill$\square$
11	
12	The key distinction is temporal. Information loss (M1) imposes a constant ceiling determined by $h$; sign misattribution (M2) is cumulative---each wrong-sign gradient step erodes policy competence below that ceiling. A wrong-sign gradient moves the optimizer \textit{away} from good policies; a reduced-magnitude gradient from discounting ($\gamma^{(N-k)}$) still points toward good policies. Sign errors produce active divergence; magnitude errors merely slow convergence (Appendix~\ref{app:sign_fundamental}).
13	
14	\textbf{Proposition 2.}\label{prop:correct_sign} \textit{Let $p$ denote the episode success rate and $K$ the number of rollouts per group. Under step-independent training with binary episode reward, the fraction of gradient updates with correct sign is:}
15	\begin{equation}
16	P(\text{correct} \mid \text{update}) = \frac{p}{1 - (1-p)^{K}}
17	\label{eq:correct_conditional}
18	\end{equation}
19	
20	\textit{Derivation.} Three mutually exclusive cases determine gradient direction for a window with locally correct actions: (1)~episode succeeded (probability $p$): correct sign; (2)~episode failed but group contains a success (probability $(1{-}p)[1{-}(1{-}p)^{K-1}]$): wrong sign; (3)~entire group failed (probability $(1{-}p)^K$): zero gradient. Conditioning on non-zero gradient (Cases 1 and 2) yields Equation~\ref{eq:correct_conditional}.
21	
22	At $p{=}12.5\%$, $K{=}8$, only 19.1\% of gradient updates have the correct sign (Figure~\ref{fig:theory_curve}). In the large-$K$ limit, $P(\text{correct} \mid \text{update}) \to p$: ${\sim}87.5\%$ of updates for locally correct windows point in the wrong direction.
23	
24	\begin{figure}[t]
25	\centering
26	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig_theory_curve.pdf}
27	\caption{Probability of correct gradient sign as a function of episode success rate $p$ (Proposition~2). At typical agent task success rates ($p < 0.2$, shaded), the majority of gradient updates carry incorrect sign regardless of group size $K$. At ALFWorld's baseline ($p{=}12.5\%$, $K{=}8$), only 19.1\% of updates are correct. The dashed diagonal shows the large-$K$ limit $P_{\text{correct}} \to p$.}
28	\label{fig:theory_curve}
29	\end{figure}
30	
31	\paragraph{Severity scales with task difficulty.} Sign misattribution becomes \textit{more} severe as the task gets harder (Equation~\ref{eq:correct_conditional}): at $p{=}50\%$, $P(\text{correct} \mid \text{update}) = 50.2\%$---barely above random; at $p{=}5\%$, only 5.1\% of updates are correct. Multi-turn agent tasks with low baseline success rates are precisely where credit assignment matters most, yet where step-independent training produces the worst gradient signal. Increasing $K$ does not help: more updates occur (fewer all-fail groups), but each remains equally likely to carry the wrong sign.
32	
33	\paragraph{Reward saturation.} When all $K$ rollouts receive the same binary reward, $\sigma = 0$ and $\hat{A}_k = 0$, producing zero gradient. At $p{=}12.5\%$, $K{=}8$, the all-fail probability is $(1{-}p)^K \approx 34.4\%$; combined with the negligible all-success case ($p^K < 10^{-7}$), roughly one-third of batches yield no gradient at all. This compounds sign misattribution: of the ${\sim}66\%$ of batches that produce a gradient, only 19.1\% have the correct sign. The effective learning rate is $0.66 \times 0.19 \approx 12.5\%$ of nominal---well below the regime where convergence guarantees hold.
34	
35	\paragraph{Cumulative divergence.} Sign misattribution produces a systematic gradient bias. Let $\theta^*$ denote an optimal policy parameterization. At each update, the expected gradient under step-independent training is:
36	\begin{equation}
37	\mathbb{E}[\nabla_\theta \mathcal{L}] = P_c \cdot g_+ + (1 - P_c) \cdot g_-
38	\label{eq:biased_gradient}
39	\end{equation}
40	where $P_c = P(\text{correct} \mid \text{update})$, $g_+$ points toward $\theta^*$, and $g_-$ points away. Empirical measurement from training logs confirms approximately comparable per-group advantage magnitudes ($|\hat{A}_{\text{correct}}|/|\hat{A}_{\text{wrong}}| \approx 1.32$). When $P_c < 0.5$ (which holds for $p < 0.5$; Equation~\ref{eq:correct_conditional}), the expected gradient points weakly away from good policies. The cumulative effect of this directional bias, amplified by the M1--M2 positive feedback loop (Section~\ref{sec:dual_mechanism}), drives progressive policy degradation: information loss (M1) caps $p$; lower $p$ increases wrong-sign frequency under M2, which further depresses $p$, producing the empirically observed collapse.
41	
```

      "content": "\\section{Theoretical Analysis}\n\\label{sec:theory}\n\nSign misattribution is the more fundamental failure mechanism because it causes active divergence, not merely slow convergence. We derive the probability of incorrect gradient direction below; full proofs appear in Appendix~\\ref{app:theory_full}.\n\n\\textbf{Definition 1} (Locally correct action). \\textit{An action $a_t$ is \\textbf{locally correct} if it would receive positive advantage under an oracle that observes the full episode history $h_t$ and has access to the true action-value function. Equivalently, window $k$ contains locally correct actions if replacing them (holding all other windows fixed) would decrease the probability of episode success.}\n\n\\textbf{Proposition 1.} \\textit{Under step-independent training with binary episode reward $R_{\\text{episode}} \\in \\{0, r_+\\}$, the probability that window $k$'s advantage $\\hat{A}_k$ has the correct sign (aligned with local action quality) approaches the base success rate $p$ as the number of rollouts $K$ per group increases.}\n\n\\textit{Proof sketch.} In a failed episode ($R_{\\text{episode}} = 0$), all windows receive $R_k = 0$. If any rollout in the group succeeded, $\\mu > 0$, so every window in the failed episode receives $\\hat{A}_k < 0$---including windows with locally correct actions. The probability of receiving the wrong sign in a failed episode is $1 - (1-p)^{K-1}$ (at least one group member succeeded). As $K$ grows, this approaches $1-p$, and the overall probability of correct sign approaches $p$. \\hfill$\\square$\n\nThe key distinction is temporal. Information loss (M1) imposes a constant ceiling determined by $h$; sign misattribution (M2) is cumulative---each wrong-sign gradient step erodes policy competence below that ceiling. A wrong-sign gradient moves the optimizer \\textit{away} from good policies; a reduced-magnitude gradient from discounting ($\\gamma^{(N-k)}$) still points toward good policies. Under step-independent training with binary episode reward, the fraction of gradient updates with correct sign is:}\n\\begin{equation}\nP(\\text{correct} \\mid \\text{update}) = \\frac{p}{1 - (1-p)^{K}}\n\\label{eq:correct_conditional}\n\\end{equation}\n\n\\textit{Derivation.} Three mutually exclusive cases determine gradient direction for a window with locally correct actions: (1)~episode succeeded (probability $p$): correct sign; (2)~episode failed but group contains a success (probability $(1{-}p)[1{-}(1{-}p)^{K-1}]$): wrong sign; (3)~entire group failed (probability $(1{-}p)^K$): zero gradient. ```text
1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	Step-independent training collapses while episode-level training succeeds; a factorial design separates the two mechanisms; and window size $h$ modulates severity.
5	
6	\subsection{Setup}
7	\label{sec:setup}
8	
9	We evaluate on ALFWorld~\cite{shridhar2021alfworld}, a text-based household environment with six task types requiring sequential reasoning over up to 50 steps. Reward is binary: $R \in \{0, r_+\}$. The base policy is Qwen3-1.7B fine-tuned on expert demonstrations (SFT baseline: 12.5\% in-distribution). At this baseline success rate, Proposition~2 predicts the majority of gradient updates carry incorrect sign. All conditions use RAGEN~\cite{ragen2025} (v0.1, commit \texttt{3a6b48b})\footnote{Our implementation extends RAGEN v0.1 with episode-level training support. Code: \url{https://github.com/RAGEN-AI/RAGEN}.} with GRPO~\cite{shao2024deepseekmath} ($K{=}8$; full hyperparameters in Appendix~\ref{app:hyperparameters}). We report success rate on a 134-task OOD set (primary) and 32-task in-distribution set (secondary). Seed counts are noted per condition.
10	
11	\subsection{Factorial Mechanism Separation}
12	\label{sec:factorial_experiments}
13	
14	We implement the factorial design from Section~\ref{sec:factorial_design} with two seeds per condition. Cell~C (full observation, step-level advantage) would isolate M2 without M1, but we encountered persistent training instability in this configuration (Appendix~\ref{app:cell_c}).
15	
16	\subsection{Results}
17	\label{sec:results}
18	
19	Cell~A collapses in two phases (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}): task success drops to 0\% while action validity initially remains high, then continued sign misattribution destroys even basic formatting.% exp_id: hsweep_h2_seed1, hsweep_h2_seed2
20	Cell~B eliminates sign misattribution while retaining information loss: training success improves, but OOD generalization is bounded by the information-loss ceiling. Both seeds converge to the same OOD ceiling despite different training trajectories, confirming the bottleneck is architectural rather than statistical.% exp_id: factorial_h2_episodeadv_seed1, factorial_h2_episodeadv_seed2
21	
22	Cell~D is the only condition that substantially exceeds the SFT baseline, with monotonic OOD improvement across both seeds.% exp_id: exp029_episode_level, exp029_seed3_ood_v2
23	
24	The A$\to$B comparison isolates M2: eliminating sign misattribution converts catastrophic collapse into bounded learning. The B$\to$D comparison isolates M1: removing the information-loss ceiling enables OOD generalization. Both mechanisms are necessary to explain the full failure pattern. Figure~\ref{fig:hsweep}(a) visualizes these trajectories.
25	
26	\begin{figure*}[t]
27	\centering
28	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{fig_training_dynamics.pdf}
29	\caption{(a)~Training dynamics across factorial conditions (metric type indicated in legend). Cell~A collapses within 5 steps; Cell~B shows bounded learning; Cell~D improves monotonically. (b)~Window size $h$ vs.\ success rate (seed~1). $h{\leq}20$ bars use a 32-task in-distribution eval set; the episode-level bar uses a 134-task OOD set (dotted separator); magnitudes are not directly comparable across eval sets. A credit assignment threshold between $h{=}10$ and $h{=}20$ separates collapse from recovery. Hatched bars indicate collapsed conditions.}
30	\label{fig:hsweep}
31	\end{figure*}
32	
33	\begin{table}[t]
34	\centering
35	\caption{1.7B results on ALFWorld (best checkpoint per seed, evaluated on held-out test sets). In-Dist: 32-task set; OOD: 134-task set. Cell~A reports best seed; Cell~B reports best checkpoint (step~5). Cell~D reports mean of two seeds (In-Dist range: 28.1--40.6; OOD range: 10.4--23.9).}
36	\label{tab:main_results}
37	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
38	\begin{tabular}{@{}llcc@{}}
39	\toprule
40	\textbf{Condition} & \textbf{Mechanisms} & \textbf{In-Dist (\%)} & \textbf{OOD (\%)} \\
41	\midrule
42	SFT baseline & --- & 12.50 & 6.72 \\
43	Cell A ($h{=}2$, step) & M1+M2 & 0 & 0 \\
44	Cell B ($h{=}2$, episode) & M1 only & 18.75 & 5.97 \\
45	Cell D (full, episode) & neither & \textbf{34.38} & \textbf{17.16} \\
46	\midrule
47	Dense reward & M1+M2 & 0 & 0 \\
48	REINFORCE++ & neither & 12.50 & --- \\
49	\bottomrule
50	\end{tabular}}
51	% exp_id: hsweep_h2_seed1, factorial_h2_episodeadv_seed1, exp029_episode_level, exp029_seed3_v2, exp013f, gae_baseline_seed1
52	\end{table}
53	
54	\paragraph{Additional conditions.} Dense reward, REINFORCE++, and discount-factor sweep experiments all confirm that sign misattribution, not reward sparsity or discounting, drives the failure (Appendix~\ref{app:additional}).
55	
56	\paragraph{Window-size sweep.} Varying window size reveals a credit assignment threshold between $h{=}10$ and $h{=}20$ that separates collapse from recovery (Figure~\ref{fig:hsweep}b; seed~1). Below this threshold, all conditions collapse to 0\%; above it, success rate increases monotonically with window size. This threshold is observed in a single seed; multi-seed verification is needed to confirm its robustness.% exp_id: h_sweep_series, exp029_seed1
57	\label{sec:results_hsweep}
58	
59	\subsection{Sign Misattribution Verification}
60	\label{sec:sign_verification}
61	
62	We operationalize local correctness (Definition~1) through episode outcome: in a successful episode, all windows with valid actions are labeled locally correct. The credit agreement rate (the fraction of such windows where GRPO's advantage sign is positive) is 52\% under step-independent training ($h{=}2$, $n{=}3{,}000$ windows), marginally above random chance. Advantage sign correlates near-perfectly with episode outcome but shows near-zero dependence on local action quality. Combined with $\gamma{=}1.0$ collapse (Appendix~\ref{app:additional}), these measurements confirm sign misattribution as the causal driver. This operationalization is a conservative approximation: successful episodes may contain suboptimal windows, making our label an upper bound on true local correctness. Even under this generous criterion, credit agreement remains near-random, strengthening the diagnosis.% exp_id: 7b_cell_a_h2
63	
64	Under pure binary reward, sign-episode correlation reaches 100\%, directly confirming M2: advantage sign perfectly tracks episode outcome (Figure~\ref{fig:sign_misattribution}). Penalty reward partially breaks this by introducing within-group variance, but credit agreement remains near-random for local quality.% exp_id: binary_credit_agreement_iter0
65	
66	\begin{figure}[t]
67	\centering
68	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig_sign_misattribution.pdf}
69	\caption{Sign-episode agreement rate under binary reward (penalty${=}0$) vs.\ penalty reward. Under binary reward, advantage sign perfectly tracks episode outcome (100\%, $n{=}157$), directly confirming M2. Penalty reward reduces this to 52.1\% ($n{=}3{,}000$), near random chance (dashed line).}
70	\label{fig:sign_misattribution}
71	\end{figure}
72	
73	\subsection{Episode-Level Training: Seed Variability}
74	\label{sec:seed_variability}
75	
76	Episode-level GRPO addresses the dual-mechanism failure, but training outcomes remain seed-dependent. We systematically evaluate $N{=}11$ random seeds under identical hyperparameters, revealing a $2{\times}2$ structure: \textit{training stability} (collapsed vs.\ non-collapsed) $\times$ \textit{OOD generalization} (stagnant vs.\ generalized).
77	
78	We define \textbf{collapsed} seeds as those where in-distribution success declines $>$50\% from peak and remains below baseline for $\geq$3 consecutive steps. \textbf{Non-collapsed} seeds are the complement. Among non-collapsed seeds, we further distinguish \textbf{generalized} (Fisher exact $p{<}0.01$ vs.\ SFT on the 134-task OOD set) from \textbf{stagnant} (not significant vs.\ SFT). Table~\ref{tab:seed_variability} reports results for each seed at its best-performing checkpoint.
79	
80	\begin{table}[t]
81	\centering
82	\caption{Episode-level GRPO across 11 seeds (134 OOD tasks, $\tau{=}0.4$, best checkpoint per seed). Category: C=Collapsed, S=Non-collapsed (stagnant), G=Generalized (Fisher exact $p{<}0.01$ vs.\ SFT). Wilson 95\% CI. SFT baseline: 6.72\% (9/134). Seed2 achieves $p{=}0.03$ (Fisher exact, two-sided), falling outside the conservative $p{<}0.01$ threshold, and is classified as Stagnant. Step-5 results for all seeds in Appendix~\ref{app:step5_unified}.}
83	\label{tab:seed_variability}
84	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%

90	seed42$^\ddagger$ & 5 & C & 12.69 & [8.0, 19.4] & NS \\

96	seed100 & 10 & S & 12.69 & [8.0, 19.4] & NS \\
97	seed2$^\ddagger$ & 15 & S & 15.67 & [10.5, 22.8] & $p{=}0.03$ \\
98	\midrule
99	seed1 & 5 & G$^\dagger$ & 23.88 & [17.4, 31.8] & $p{<}0.001$ \\
100	seed5 & 10 & G & 29.10 & [22.1, 37.3] & $p{<}0.001$ \\
101	seed6 & 15 & G & 29.10 & [22.2, 37.1] & $p{<}0.001$ \\

111	
112	Three distinct outcomes emerge (Table~\ref{tab:seed_variability}): roughly a quarter of seeds collapse with response length inflation (Appendix~\ref{app:response_length}), a third are stagnant with OOD performance indistinguishable from SFT, and a third generalize with significant OOD improvement. No collapsed seed generalizes, and training duration does not explain the gap between stagnant and generalized seeds. Stable training is necessary but not sufficient; the mechanism driving seed-dependent transfer remains an open question (Section~\ref{sec:discussion}).% exp_id: exp029 multi-seed series
113	
114	\subsection{Cross-Scale and Cross-Environment Verification}
115	\label{sec:cross_scale}
116	
117	A single-seed experiment on Qwen3-8B provides preliminary evidence that both failure and fix persist at scale. Cell~A collapses within two steps; Cell~D reaches 90.3\% OOD, substantially exceeding the SFT baseline (full results in Appendix~\ref{app:8b_results}). Restricting context to $h{=}2$ produces a large M1 gap, confirming that information loss remains severe at scale. Single-seed due to memory constraints.% exp_id: 8b_cell_c_episode_v9d, 8b_v9d_ood_eval_v5
118	Step-independent training also collapses on ScienceWorld~\cite{wang2022scienceworld}: Cell~A drops to 0\% throughout all 15 steps. Restricting context to $h{=}2$ produces a comparable M1 gap to the 8B ALFWorld setting, suggesting the pathology is environment-general.% exp_id: scienceworld_grpo_cell_a, sw_info_loss_bjb1

Both seeds converge to the same OOD ceiling despite different training trajectories, confirming the bottleneck is architectural rather than statistical.% exp_id: factorial_h2_episodeadv_seed1, factorial_h2_episodeadv_seed2\n\nCell~D is the only condition that substantially exceeds the SFT baseline, with monotonic OOD improvement across both seeds.% exp_id: exp029_episode_level, exp029_seed3_ood_v2\n\nThe A$\\to$B comparison isolates M2: eliminating sign misattribution converts catastrophic collapse into bounded learning. The B$\\to$D comparison isolates M1: removing the information-loss ceiling enables OOD generalization. Both mechanisms are necessary to explain the full failure pattern. Figure~\\ref{fig:hsweep}(a) visualizes these trajectories.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{fig_training_dynamics.pdf}\n\\caption{(a)~Training dynamics across factorial conditions (metric type indicated in legend). Cell~A collapses within 5 steps; Cell~B shows bounded learning; Cell~D improves monotonically. (b)~Window size $h$ vs.\\ success rate (seed~1). $h{\\leq}20$ bars use a 32-task in-distribution eval set; the episode-level bar uses a 134-task OOD set (dotted separator); magnitudes are not directly comparable across eval sets. A credit assignment threshold between $h{=}10$ and $h{=}20$ separates collapse from recovery. Hatched bars indicate collapsed conditions.}\n\\label{fig:hsweep}\n\\end{figure*}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{1.7B results on ALFWorld (best checkpoint per seed, evaluated on held-out test sets). In-Dist: 32-task set; OOD: 134-task set. Cell~A reports best seed; Cell~B reports best checkpoint (step~5). Cell~D reports mean of two seeds (In-Dist range: 28.1--40.6; OOD range: 10.4--23.9).}\n\\label{tab:main_results}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcc@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Mechanisms} & \\textbf{In-Dist (\\%)} & \\textbf{OOD (\\%)} \\\\\n\\midrule\nSFT baseline & --- & 12.50 & 6.72 \\\\\nCell A ($h{=}2$, step) & M1+M2 & 0 & 0 \\\\\nCell B ($h{=}2$, episode) & M1 only & 18.75 & 5.97 \\\\\nCell D (full, episode) & neither & \\textbf{34.38} & \\textbf{17.16} \\\\\n\\midrule\nDense reward & M1+M2 & 0 & 0 \\\\\nREINFORCE++ & neither & 12.50 & --- \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n% exp_id: hsweep_h2_seed1, factorial_h2_episodeadv_seed1, exp029_episode_level, exp029_seed3_v2, exp013f, gae_baseline_seed1\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Additional conditions.} Dense reward, REINFORCE++, and discount-factor sweep experiments all confirm that sign misattribution, not reward sparsity or discounting, drives the failure (Appendix~\\ref{app:additional}).\n\n\\paragraph{Window-size sweep.} Varying window size reveals a credit assignment threshold between $h{=}10$ and $h{=}20$ that separates collapse from recovery (Figure~\\ref{fig:hsweep}b; seed~1). Below this threshold, all conditions collapse to 0\\%; above it, success rate increases monotonically with window size. This threshold is observed in a single seed; multi-seed verification is needed to confirm its robustness.% exp_id: h_sweep_series, exp029_seed1\n\\label{sec:results_hsweep}\n\n\\subsection{Sign Misattribution Verification}\n\\label{sec:sign_verification}\n\nWe operationalize local correctness (Definition~1) through episode outcome: in a successful episode, all windows with valid actions are labeled locally correct. The credit agreement rate (the fraction of such windows where GRPO's advantage sign is positive) is 52\\% under step-independent training ($h{=}2$, $n{=}3{,}000$ windows), marginally above random chance. Advantage sign correlates near-perfectly with episode outcome but shows near-zero dependence on local action quality. Combined with $\\gamma{=}1.0$ collapse (Appendix~\\ref{app:additional}), these measurements confirm sign misattribution as the causal driver. This operationalization is a conservative approximation: successful episodes may contain suboptimal windows, making our label an upper bound on true local correctness. Even under this generous criterion, credit agreement remains near-random, strengthening the diagnosis.% exp_id: 7b_cell_a_h2\n\nUnder pure binary reward, sign-episode correlation reaches 100\\%, directly confirming M2: advantage sign perfectly tracks episode outcome (Figure~\\ref{fig:sign_misattribution}). Penalty reward partially breaks this by introducing within-group variance, but credit agreement remains near-random for local quality.% exp_id: binary_credit_agreement_iter0\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{fig_sign_misattribution.pdf}\n\\caption{Sign-episode agreement rate under binary reward (penalty${=}0$) vs.\\ penalty reward. Under binary reward, advantage sign perfectly tracks episode outcome (100\\%, $n{=}157$), directly confirming M2. Penalty reward reduces this to 52.1\\% ($n{=}3{,}000$), near random chance (dashed line).}\n\\label{fig:sign_misattribution}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\subsection{Episode-Level Training: Seed Variability}\n\\label{sec:seed_variability}\n\nEpisode-level GRPO addresses the dual-mechanism failure, but training outcomes remain seed-dependent. We systematically evaluate $N{=}11$ random seeds under identical hyperparameters, revealing a $2{\\times}2$ structure: \\textit{training stability} (collapsed vs.\\ non-collapsed) $\\times$ \\textit{OOD generalization} (stagnant vs.\\ generalized).\n\nWe define \\textbf{collapsed} seeds as those where in-distribution success declines $>$50\\% from peak and remains below baseline for $\\geq$3 consecutive steps. \\textbf{Non-collapsed} seeds are the complement. Among non-collapsed seeds, we further distinguish \\textbf{generalized} (Fisher exact $p{<}0.01$ vs.\\ SFT on the 134-task OOD set) from \\textbf{stagnant} (not significant vs.\\ SFT). Table~\\ref{tab:seed_variability} reports results for each seed at its best-performing checkpoint.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Episode-level GRPO across 11 seeds (134 OOD tasks, $\\tau{=}0.4$, best checkpoint per seed). Category: C=Collapsed, S=Non-collapsed (stagnant), G=Generalized (Fisher exact $p{<}0.01$ vs.\\ SFT). Wilson 95\\% CI. SFT baseline: 6.72\\% (9/134). Seed2 achieves $p{=}0.03$ (Fisher exact, two-sided), falling outside the conservative $p{<}0.01$ threshold, and is classified as Stagnant. Step-5 results for all seeds in Appendix~\\ref{app:step5_unified}.}\n\\label{tab:seed_variability}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Seed} & \\textbf{Step} & \\textbf{Cat.} & \\textbf{OOD (\\%)} & \\textbf{95\\% CI} & \\textbf{vs SFT} \\\\\n\\midrule\nseed2048$^\\ddagger$ & 5 & C & 8.21 & [4.6, 14.2] & NS \\\\\nseed42$^\\ddagger$ & 5 & C & 12.69 & [8.0, 19.4] & NS \\\\\nseed8 & 5 & C & 11.94 & [7.5, 18.5] & NS \\\\\n\\midrule\nseed200 & 5 & S & 10.45 & [6.3, 16.8] & NS \\\\\nseed300 & 5 & S & 10.45 & [6.3, 16.8] & NS \\\\\nseed100 & 5 & S & 11.19 & [6.9, 17.6] & NS \\\\\nseed100 & 10 & S & 12.69 & [8.0, 19.4] & NS \\\\\nseed2$^\\ddagger$ & 15 & S & 15.67 & [10.5, 22.8] & $p{=}0.03$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nseed1 & 5 & G$^\\dagger$ & 23.88 & [17.4, 31.8] & $p{<}0.001$ \\\\\nseed5 & 10 & G & 29.10 & [22.1, 37.3] & $p{<}0.001$ \\\\\nseed6 & 15 & G & 29.10 & [22.2, 37.1] & $p{<}0.001$ \\\\\nseed4 & 15 & G & 30.60 & [23.5, 38.7] & $p{<}0.001$ \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\begin{flushleft}\n\\small $^\\dagger$ RAGEN built-in eval; checkpoint unavailable for re-evaluation. \\\\\n\\small $^\\ddagger$ OOD evaluated via RAGEN built-in eval; not independently re-evaluated under D134 protocol. \\\\\n\\small All results are single-run evaluations at $\\tau{=}0.4$; see Limitations for eval variance discussion.\n\\end{flushleft}\n\\end{table}\n\nThree distinct outcomes emerge (Table~\\ref{tab:seed_variability}): roughly a quarter of seeds collapse with response length inflation (Appendix~\\ref{app:response_length}), a third are stagnant with OOD performance indistinguishable from SFT, and a third generalize with significant OOD improvement. No collapsed seed generalizes, and training duration does not explain the gap between stagnant and generalized seeds. Stable training is necessary but not sufficient; the mechanism driving seed-dependent transfer remains an open question (Section~\\ref{sec:discussion}).% exp_id: exp029 multi-seed series\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Scale and Cross-Environment Verification}\n\\label{sec:cross_scale}\n\nA single-seed experiment on Qwen3-8B provides preliminary evidence that both failure and fix persist at scale. Cell~A collapses within two steps; Cell~D reaches 90.3\\% OOD, substantially exceeding the SFT baseline (full results in Appendix~\\ref{app:8b_results}). Restricting context to $h{=}2$ produces a large M1 gap, confirming that information loss remains severe at scale. Single-seed due to memory constraints.% exp_id: 8b_cell_c_episode_v9d, 8b_v9d_ood_eval_v5\nStep-independent training also collapses on ScienceWorld~\\cite{wang2022scienceworld}: Cell~A drops to 0\\% throughout all 15 steps. Restricting context to $h{=}2$ produces a comparable M1 gap to the 8B ALFWorld setting, suggesting the pathology is environment-general.% exp_id: scienceworld_grpo_cell_a, sw_info_loss_bjb1\n",

    "oldString": "\\paragraph{Severity scales with task difficulty.} Sign misattribution becomes \\textit{more} severe as the task gets harder (Equation~\\ref{eq:correct_conditional}): at $p{=}50\\%$, $P(\\text{correct} \\mid \\text{update}) = 50.2\\%$---barely above random; at $p{=}5\\%$, only 5.1\\% of updates are correct.",
    "newString": "\\paragraph{Severity scales with task difficulty.} Sign misattribution becomes \\textit{more} severe as the task gets harder (Equation~\\ref{eq:correct_conditional}): at $p{=}50\\%$, $P(\\text{correct} \\mid \\text{update}) \\leq 50.2\\%$; at $p{=}5\\%$, at most 5.1\\% of updates are correct.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Theoretical Analysis}\n\\label{sec:theory}\n\nSign misattribution is the more fundamental failure mechanism because it causes active divergence, not merely slow convergence. We derive the probability of incorrect gradient direction below; full proofs appear in Appendix~\\ref{app:theory_full}.\n\n\\textbf{Definition 1} (Locally correct action). \\textit{An action $a_t$ is \\textbf{locally correct} if it would receive positive advantage under an oracle that observes the full episode history $h_t$ and has access to the true action-value function. Equivalently, window $k$ contains locally correct actions if replacing them (holding all other windows fixed) would decrease the probability of episode success.}\n\n\\textbf{Proposition 1.} \\textit{Under step-independent training with binary episode reward $R_{\\text{episode}} \\in \\{0, r_+\\}$, the probability that window $k$'s advantage $\\hat{A}_k$ has the correct sign (aligned with local action quality) approaches the base success rate $p$ as the number of rollouts $K$ per group increases.}\n\n\\textit{Proof sketch.} In a failed episode ($R_{\\text{episode}} = 0$), all windows receive $R_k = 0$. If any rollout in the group succeeded, $\\mu > 0$, so every window in the failed episode receives $\\hat{A}_k < 0$---including windows with locally correct actions. The probability of receiving the wrong sign in a failed episode is $1 - (1-p)^{K-1}$ (at least one group member succeeded). As $K$ grows, this approaches $1-p$, and the overall probability of correct sign approaches $p$. \\hfill$\\square$\n\nThe key distinction is temporal. Information loss (M1) imposes a constant ceiling determined by $h$; sign misattribution (M2) is cumulative---each wrong-sign gradient step erodes policy competence below that ceiling. A wrong-sign gradient moves the optimizer \\textit{away} from good policies; a reduced-magnitude gradient from discounting ($\\gamma^{(N-k)}$) still points toward good policies. Sign errors produce active divergence; magnitude errors merely slow convergence (Appendix~\\ref{app:sign_fundamental}).\n\n\\textbf{Proposition 2.}\\label{prop:correct_sign} \\textit{Let $p$ denote the episode success rate and $K$ the number of rollouts per group. Under step-independent training with binary episode reward, the fraction of gradient updates with correct sign is upper bounded by:}\n\\begin{equation}\nP(\\text{correct} \\mid \\text{update}) \\leq \\frac{p}{1 - (1-p)^{K}}\n\\label{eq:correct_conditional}\n\\end{equation}\n\n\\textit{Derivation.} Three mutually exclusive cases determine gradient direction for a window with locally correct actions: (1)~episode succeeded (probability $p$): correct sign; (2)~episode failed but group contains a success (probability $(1{-}p)[1{-}(1{-}p)^{K-1}]$): wrong sign; (3)~entire group failed (probability $(1{-}p)^K$): zero gradient. Conditioning on non-zero gradient (Cases 1 and 2) yields Equation~\\ref{eq:correct_conditional}. The bound is tight when episode success implies all constituent windows are locally correct; in practice, successful episodes may contain suboptimal windows, so the true fraction of correctly signed updates may be lower.\n\nAt $p{=}12.5\\%$, $K{=}8$, this yields an upper bound of 19.1\\% on correctly signed gradient updates (Figure~\\ref{fig:theory_curve}). In the large-$K$ limit, $P(\\text{correct} \\mid \\text{update}) \\to p$: ${\\sim}87.5\\%$ of updates for locally correct windows point in the wrong direction.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{fig_theory_curve.pdf}\n\\caption{Upper bound on the probability of correct gradient sign as a function of episode success rate $p$ (Proposition~2). At typical agent task success rates ($p < 0.2$, shaded), the majority of gradient updates carry incorrect sign regardless of group size $K$. At ALFWorld's baseline ($p{=}12.5\\%$, $K{=}8$), at most 19.1\\% of updates are correct. The dashed diagonal shows the large-$K$ limit $P_{\\text{correct}} \\to p$.}\n\\label{fig:theory_curve}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{Severity scales with task difficulty.} Sign misattribution becomes \\textit{more} severe as the task gets harder (Equation~\\ref{eq:correct_conditional}): at $p{=}50\\%$, $P(\\text{correct} \\mid \\text{update}) = 50.2\\%$---barely above random; at $p{=}5\\%$, only 5.1\\% of updates are correct. Multi-turn agent tasks with low baseline success rates are precisely where credit assignment matters most, yet where step-independent training produces the worst gradient signal. Increasing $K$ does not help: more updates occur (fewer all-fail groups), but each remains equally likely to carry the wrong sign.\n\n\\paragraph{Reward saturation.} When all $K$ rollouts receive the same binary reward, $\\sigma = 0$ and $\\hat{A}_k = 0$, producing zero gradient. At $p{=}12.5\\%$, $K{=}8$, the all-fail probability is $(1{-}p)^K \\approx 34.4\\%$; combined with the negligible all-success case ($p^K < 10^{-7}$), roughly one-third of batches yield no gradient at all. This compounds sign misattribution: of the ${\\sim}66\\%$ of batches that produce a gradient, only 19.1\\% have the correct sign. The effective learning rate is $0.66 \\times 0.19 \\approx 12.5\\%$ of nominal---well below the regime where convergence guarantees hold.\n\n\\paragraph{Cumulative divergence.} Sign misattribution produces a systematic gradient bias. Let $\\theta^*$ denote an optimal policy parameterization. At each update, the expected gradient under step-independent training is:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\mathbb{E}[\\nabla_\\theta \\mathcal{L}] = P_c \\cdot g_+ + (1 - P_c) \\cdot g_-\n\\label{eq:biased_gradient}\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $P_c = P(\\text{correct} \\mid \\text{update})$, $g_+$ points toward $\\theta^*$, and $g_-$ points away. Empirical measurement from training logs confirms approximately comparable per-group advantage magnitudes ($|\\hat{A}_{\\text{correct}}|/|\\hat{A}_{\\text{wrong}}| \\approx 1.32$). When $P_c < 0.5$ (which holds for $p < 0.5$; Equation~\\ref{eq:correct_conditional}), the expected gradient points weakly away from good policies. The cumulative effect of this directional bias, amplified by the M1--M2 positive feedback loop (Section~\\ref{sec:dual_mechanism}), drives progressive policy degradation: information loss (M1) caps $p$; lower $p$ increases wrong-sign frequency under M2, which further depresses $p$, producing the empirically observed collapse.\n",

Under step-independent training with binary episode reward, the fraction of gradient updates with correct sign is upper bounded by:}\n\\begin{equation}\nP(\\text{correct} \\mid \\text{update}) \\leq \\frac{p}{1 - (1-p)^{K}}\n\\label{eq:correct_conditional}\n\\end{equation}\n\n\\textit{Derivation.} Three mutually exclusive cases determine gradient direction for a window with locally correct actions: (1)~episode succeeded (probability $p$): correct sign; (2)~episode failed but group contains a success (probability $(1{-}p)[1{-}(1{-}p)^{K-1}]$): wrong sign; (3)~entire group failed (probability $(1{-}p)^K$): zero gradient. Conditioning on non-zero gradient (Cases 1 and 2) yields Equation~\\ref{eq:correct_conditional}.\n\nAt $p{=}12.5\\%$, $K{=}8$, only 19.1\\% of gradient updates have the correct sign (Figure~\\ref{fig:theory_curve}). In the large-$K$ limit, $P(\\text{correct} \\mid \\text{update}) \\to p$: ${\\sim}87.5\\%$ of updates for locally correct windows point in the wrong direction.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{fig_theory_curve.pdf}\n\\caption{Probability of correct gradient sign as a function of episode success rate $p$ (Proposition~2). At typical agent task success rates ($p < 0.2$, shaded), the majority of gradient updates carry incorrect sign regardless of group size $K$. At ALFWorld's baseline ($p{=}12.5\\%$, $K{=}8$), only 19.1\\% of updates are correct. The dashed diagonal shows the large-$K$ limit $P_{\\text{correct}} \\to p$.}\n\\label{fig:theory_curve}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{Severity scales with task difficulty.} Sign misattribution becomes \\textit{more} severe as the task gets harder (Equation~\\ref{eq:correct_conditional}): at $p{=}50\\%$, $P(\\text{correct} \\mid \\text{update}) = 50.2\\%$---barely above random; at $p{=}5\\%$, only 5.1\\% of updates are correct. Multi-turn agent tasks with low baseline success rates are precisely where credit assignment matters most, yet where step-independent training produces the worst gradient signal. Increasing $K$ does not help: more updates occur (fewer all-fail groups), but each remains equally likely to carry the wrong sign.\n\n\\paragraph{Reward saturation.} When all $K$ rollouts receive the same binary reward, $\\sigma = 0$ and $\\hat{A}_k = 0$, producing zero gradient. At $p{=}12.5\\%$, $K{=}8$, the all-fail probability is $(1{-}p)^K \\approx 34.4\\%$; combined with the negligible all-success case ($p^K < 10^{-7}$), roughly one-third of batches yield no gradient at all. This compounds sign misattribution: of the ${\\sim}66\\%$ of batches that produce a gradient, only 19.1\\% have the correct sign. The effective learning rate is $0.66 \\times 0.19 \\approx 12.5\\%$ of nominal---well below the regime where convergence guarantees hold.\n\n\\paragraph{Cumulative divergence.} Sign misattribution produces a systematic gradient bias. Let $\\theta^*$ denote an optimal policy parameterization. At each update, the expected gradient under step-independent training is:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\mathbb{E}[\\nabla_\\theta \\mathcal{L}] = P_c \\cdot g_+ + (1 - P_c) \\cdot g_-\n\\label{eq:biased_gradient}\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $P_c = P(\\text{correct} \\mid \\text{update})$, $g_+$ points toward $\\theta^*$, and $g_-$ points away. Empirical measurement from training logs confirms approximately comparable per-group advantage magnitudes ($|\\hat{A}_{\\text{correct}}|/|\\hat{A}_{\\text{wrong}}| \\approx 1.32$). When $P_c < 0.5$ (which holds for $p < 0.5$; Equation~\\ref{eq:correct_conditional}), the expected gradient points weakly away from good policies. The cumulative effect of this directional bias, amplified by the M1--M2 positive feedback loop (Section~\\ref{sec:dual_mechanism}), drives progressive policy degradation: information loss (M1) caps $p$; lower $p$ increases wrong-sign frequency under M2, which further depresses $p$, producing the empirically observed collapse.\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Theoretical Analysis}\n\\label{sec:theory}\n\nSign misattribution is the more fundamental failure mechanism because it causes active divergence, not merely slow convergence. We derive the probability of incorrect gradient direction below; full proofs appear in Appendix~\\ref{app:theory_full}.\n\n\\textbf{Definition 1} (Locally correct action). \\textit{An action $a_t$ is \\textbf{locally correct} if it would receive positive advantage under an oracle that observes the full episode history $h_t$ and has access to the true action-value function. Equivalently, window $k$ contains locally correct actions if replacing them (holding all other windows fixed) would decrease the probability of episode success.}\n\n\\textbf{Proposition 1.} \\textit{Under step-independent training with binary episode reward $R_{\\text{episode}} \\in \\{0, r_+\\}$, the probability that window $k$'s advantage $\\hat{A}_k$ has the correct sign (aligned with local action quality) approaches the base success rate $p$ as the number of rollouts $K$ per group increases.}\n\n\\textit{Proof sketch.} In a failed episode ($R_{\\text{episode}} = 0$), all windows receive $R_k = 0$. If any rollout in the group succeeded, $\\mu > 0$, so every window in the failed episode receives $\\hat{A}_k < 0$---including windows with locally correct actions. The probability of receiving the wrong sign in a failed episode is $1 - (1-p)^{K-1}$ (at least one group member succeeded). As $K$ grows, this approaches $1-p$, and the overall probability of correct sign approaches $p$. \\hfill$\\square$\n\nThe key distinction is temporal.     "originalFile": "\\section{Theoretical Analysis}\n\\label{sec:theory}\n\nSign misattribution is the more fundamental failure mechanism because it causes active divergence, not merely slow convergence. We derive the probability of incorrect gradient direction below; full proofs appear in Appendix~\\ref{app:theory_full}.\n\n\\textbf{Definition 1} (Locally correct action). \\textit{An action $a_t$ is \\textbf{locally correct} if it would receive positive advantage under an oracle that observes the full episode history $h_t$ and has access to the true action-value function. Equivalently, window $k$ contains locally correct actions if replacing them (holding all other windows fixed) would decrease the probability of episode success.}\n\n\\textbf{Proposition 1.} \\textit{Under step-independent training with binary episode reward $R_{\\text{episode}} \\in \\{0, r_+\\}$, the probability that window $k$'s advantage $\\hat{A}_k$ has the correct sign (aligned with local action quality) approaches the base success rate $p$ as the number of rollouts $K$ per group increases.}\n\n\\textit{Proof sketch.} In a failed episode ($R_{\\text{episode}} = 0$), all windows receive $R_k = 0$. If any rollout in the group succeeded, $\\mu > 0$, so every window in the failed episode receives $\\hat{A}_k < 0$---including windows with locally correct actions. The probability of receiving the wrong sign in a failed episode is $1 - (1-p)^{K-1}$ (at least one group member succeeded). As $K$ grows, this approaches $1-p$, and the overall probability of correct sign approaches $p$. \\hfill$\\square$\n\nThe key distinction is temporal. Information loss (M1) imposes a constant ceiling determined by $h$; sign misattribution (M2) is cumulative---each wrong-sign gradient step erodes policy competence below that ceiling. A wrong-sign gradient moves the optimizer \\textit{away} from good policies; a reduced-magnitude gradient from discounting ($\\gamma^{(N-k)}$) still points toward good policies. Sign errors produce active divergence; magnitude errors merely slow convergence (Appendix~\\ref{app:sign_fundamental}).\n\n\\textbf{Proposition 2.}\\label{prop:correct_sign} \\textit{Let $p$ denote the episode success rate and $K$ the number of rollouts per group. Under step-independent training with binary episode reward, the fraction of gradient updates with correct sign is upper bounded by:}\n\\begin{equation}\nP(\\text{correct} \\mid \\text{update}) \\leq \\frac{p}{1 - (1-p)^{K}}\n\\label{eq:correct_conditional}\n\\end{equation}\n\n\\textit{Derivation.} Three mutually exclusive cases determine gradient direction for a window with locally correct actions: (1)~episode succeeded (probability $p$): correct sign; (2)~episode failed but group contains a success (probability $(1{-}p)[1{-}(1{-}p)^{K-1}]$): wrong sign; (3)~entire group failed (probability $(1{-}p)^K$): zero gradient. Conditioning on non-zero gradient (Cases 1 and 2) yields Equation~\\ref{eq:correct_conditional}. The bound is tight when episode success implies all constituent windows are locally correct; in practice, successful episodes may contain suboptimal windows, so the true fraction of correctly signed updates may be lower.\n\nAt $p{=}12.5\\%$, $K{=}8$, this yields an upper bound of 19.1\\% on correctly signed gradient updates (Figure~\\ref{fig:theory_curve}). In the large-$K$ limit, $P(\\text{correct} \\mid \\text{update}) \\to p$: ${\\sim}87.5\\%$ of updates for locally correct windows point in the wrong direction.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{fig_theory_curve.pdf}\n\\caption{Upper bound on the probability of correct gradient sign as a function of episode success rate $p$ (Proposition~2). At typical agent task success rates ($p < 0.2$, shaded), the majority of gradient updates carry incorrect sign regardless of group size $K$. At ALFWorld's baseline ($p{=}12.5\\%$, $K{=}8$), at most 19.1\\% of updates are correct. The dashed diagonal shows the large-$K$ limit $P_{\\text{correct}} \\to p$.}\n\\label{fig:theory_curve}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{Severity scales with task difficulty.} Sign misattribution becomes \\textit{more} severe as the task gets harder (Equation~\\ref{eq:correct_conditional}): at $p{=}50\\%$, $P(\\text{correct} \\mid \\text{update}) \\leq 50.2\\%$; at $p{=}5\\%$, at most 5.1\\% of updates are correct. Multi-turn agent tasks with low baseline success rates are precisely where credit assignment matters most, yet where step-independent training produces the worst gradient signal. Increasing $K$ does not help: more updates occur (fewer all-fail groups), but each remains equally likely to carry the wrong sign.\n\n\\paragraph{Reward saturation.} When all $K$ rollouts receive the same binary reward, $\\sigma = 0$ and $\\hat{A}_k = 0$, producing zero gradient. At $p{=}12.5\\%$, $K{=}8$, the all-fail probability is $(1{-}p)^K \\approx 34.4\\%$; combined with the negligible all-success case ($p^K < 10^{-7}$), roughly one-third of batches yield no gradient at all. This compounds sign misattribution: of the ${\\sim}66\\%$ of batches that produce a gradient, only 19.1\\% have the correct sign. The effective learning rate is $0.66 \\times 0.19 \\approx 12.5\\%$ of nominal---well below the regime where convergence guarantees hold.\n\n\\paragraph{Cumulative divergence.} Sign misattribution produces a systematic gradient bias. Let $\\theta^*$ denote an optimal policy parameterization. At each update, the expected gradient under step-independent training is:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\mathbb{E}[\\nabla_\\theta \\mathcal{L}] = P_c \\cdot g_+ + (1 - P_c) \\cdot g_-\n\\label{eq:biased_gradient}\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $P_c = P(\\text{correct} \\mid \\text{update})$, $g_+$ points toward $\\theta^*$, and $g_-$ points away. Empirical measurement from training logs confirms approximately comparable per-group advantage magnitudes ($|\\hat{A}_{\\text{correct}}|/|\\hat{A}_{\\text{wrong}}| \\approx 1.32$; measured from a single training snapshot; validation across seeds and training stages would strengthen this estimate). When $P_c < 0.5$ (which holds for $p < 0.5$; Equation~\\ref{eq:correct_conditional}), the expected gradient points weakly away from good policies. The cumulative effect of this directional bias, amplified by the M1--M2 positive feedback loop (Section~\\ref{sec:dual_mechanism}), drives progressive policy degradation: information loss (M1) caps $p$; lower $p$ increases wrong-sign frequency under M2, which further depresses $p$, producing the empirically observed collapse.\n",

  "old_string": "We evaluate on ALFWorld~\\cite{shridhar2021alfworld}, a text-based household environment with six task types requiring sequential reasoning over up to 50 steps. Reward is binary: $R \\in \\{0, r_+\\}$. The base policy is Qwen3-1.7B fine-tuned on expert demonstrations (SFT baseline: 12.5\\% in-distribution). At this baseline success rate, Proposition~2 predicts the majority of gradient updates carry incorrect sign. All conditions use RAGEN~\\cite{ragen2025} (v0.1, commit \\texttt{3a6b48b})\\footnote{Our implementation extends RAGEN v0.1 with episode-level training support. Code: \\url{https://github.com/RAGEN-AI/RAGEN}.} with GRPO~\\cite{shao2024deepseekmath} ($K{=}8$; full hyperparameters in Appendix~\\ref{app:hyperparameters}). We report success rate on a 134-task OOD set (primary) and 32-task in-distribution set (secondary). Seed counts are noted per condition.",
  "new_string": "We evaluate on ALFWorld~\\cite{shridhar2021alfworld}, a text-based household environment with six task types requiring sequential reasoning over up to 50 steps. The environment provides ternary reward: $R \\in \\{r_-, 0, r_+\\}$, where $r_- = -0.1$ per invalid action, $0$ for valid but unsuccessful actions, and $r_+$ for task success. Our theoretical analysis (Section~\\ref{sec:theory}) assumes the simplified binary case ($R \\in \\{0, r_+\\}$), which yields an upper bound on sign misattribution severity; Section~\\ref{sec:sign_verification} validates this bound under both reward structures. The base policy is Qwen3-1.7B fine-tuned on expert demonstrations (SFT baseline: 12.5\\% in-distribution). At this baseline success rate, Proposition~2 predicts the majority of gradient updates carry incorrect sign. All conditions use RAGEN~\\cite{ragen2025} (v0.1, commit \\texttt{3a6b48b})\\footnote{Our implementation extends RAGEN v0.1 with episode-level training support. Code available upon acceptance.} with GRPO~\\cite{shao2024deepseekmath} ($K{=}8$; full hyperparameters in Appendix~\\ref{app:hyperparameters}). We report success rate on a 134-task OOD set (primary) and 32-task in-distribution set (secondary). Seed counts are noted per condition."
}

}

<details open><summary>🔧 Tool call — Edit · `toolu_019zVeXhRuxN9RPaCYZVVwXc`</summary>

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_1/projects/cost-verify-agent-rl/docs/paper/experiments.tex",
  "old_string": "\\subsection{Sign Misattribution Verification}\n\\label{sec:sign_verification}\n\nWe operationalize local correctness (Definition~1) through episode outcome: in a successful episode, all windows with valid actions are labeled locally correct. The credit agreement rate (the fraction of such windows where GRPO's advantage sign is positive) is 52\\% under step-independent training ($h{=}2$, $n{=}3{,}000$ windows), marginally above random chance. Advantage sign correlates near-perfectly with episode outcome but shows near-zero dependence on local action quality. Combined with $\\gamma{=}1.0$ collapse (Appendix~\\ref{app:additional}), these measurements confirm sign misattribution as the causal driver. This operationalization is a conservative approximation: successful episodes may contain suboptimal windows, making our label an upper bound on true local correctness. Even under this generous criterion, credit agreement remains near-random, strengthening the diagnosis.% exp_id: 7b_cell_a_h2\n\nUnder pure binary reward, sign-episode correlation reaches 100\\%, directly confirming M2: advantage sign perfectly tracks episode outcome (Figure~\\ref{fig:sign_misattribution}). Penalty reward partially breaks this by introducing within-group variance, but credit agreement remains near-random for local quality.% exp_id: binary_credit_agreement_iter0",
  "new_string": "\\subsection{Sign Misattribution Verification}\n\\label{sec:sign_verification}\n\nWe distinguish three metrics that each capture a different aspect of credit assignment quality:\n\n\\textit{(1) Sign-episode correlation}: whether advantage sign tracks episode outcome (success/failure). Under pure binary reward (penalty${=}0$), this correlation is 100\\% ($n{=}157$ windows; Figure~\\ref{fig:sign_misattribution}), directly confirming M2: advantage sign is entirely determined by episode outcome, not local action quality. This is by construction, since all windows in a successful episode share the same positive return under binary reward.% exp_id: binary_credit_agreement_iter0\n\n\\textit{(2) Credit agreement rate}: whether advantage sign matches local action quality, operationalized through action validity as a proxy for local correctness (Definition~1). Under step-independent training with penalty reward ($h{=}2$, $n{=}3{,}000$ windows), only 52.07\\% of windows with valid actions receive positive advantage, marginally above random chance ($p{=}0.025$ vs.\\ 50\\%; $+$2.07pp effect size). This near-random credit agreement shows that advantage sign carries almost no information about local action quality. Penalty reward partially breaks sign-episode correlation by introducing within-group variance, but the resulting signal remains too weak for meaningful credit assignment. This operationalization is conservative: successful episodes may contain suboptimal windows, making our validity label an upper bound on true local correctness. Even under this generous criterion, credit agreement remains near-random, strengthening the diagnosis.% exp_id: 7b_cell_a_h2\n\n\\textit{(3) $P(\\text{correct} \\mid \\text{update})$}: the theoretical upper bound from Proposition~2 ($\\leq$19.1\\% at $p{=}12.5\\%$, $K{=}8$). This bounds the fraction of non-zero gradient updates with correct sign, a different quantity from the credit agreement rate, which conditions on action validity rather than gradient participation. The two metrics are complementary: Proposition~2 bounds worst-case gradient quality; credit agreement measures empirical signal strength.\n\nCombined with $\\gamma{=}1.0$ collapse (Appendix~\\ref{app:additional}), these measurements confirm sign misattribution as the causal driver of training failure."
}
```

{
